It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.1, F40.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because... well, the thought of seeing a rat has just gotten so bad lately. I work as a building cleaner, and I can’t even do parts of my job anymore. Even when I know it’s just an old building or a trash room, and there's probably nothing actually dangerous there, I just... I can't do it. The fear is just too much.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of the rat itself or more about the possibility of getting sick or something bad happening if you see one?\n\nPatient: I think it's really just the rat itself. I don't... I don't usually think about getting sick or catching anything from them. It's just the animal. The way they look, the way they move... just the thought of seeing one right in front of me is what terrifies me. I'm not really worried about what it's going to do to me, it's just the rat.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of other animals that are similar to rats, like mice?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I see mice sometimes down in the basements, and... well, I don't love them, but they don't bother me like that. I can keep working if I see a mouse. It's really just rats. I don't know why, but a mouse just doesn't give me that same terrifying feeling. It's strictly rats.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats started to affect other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it's just really messing up my job. I mean... I've actually had to refuse or put off cleaning assignments in certain buildings because of it. My supervisor has noticed, and he's started questioning if I can even be counted on for my regular routes anymore. I'm just really worried this is going to cost me getting better shifts, or... or a chance at a lead position. So, it's really my work life and my livelihood that are taking the biggest hit right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been afraid of rats?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two years now. It started right around the time I moved into this older apartment building. People living there just kept talking about rats being around the place, and... yeah, that's when this whole thing really began.\n\nDoctor: Can seeing pictures or videos of rats, or even just thinking about them when you're not actually around them, trigger this fear for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't like looking at them, but pictures or thinking about them doesn't give me that same panic. It's really only when I see a real one, or think there's one actually in the room with me, that I get so scared.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your social life or relationships with family and friends in any way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't really caused any problems with my friends or family. It's really just work where it's a big issue. Like I said, it's the buildings I have to clean that are the problem. My social life is pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats or worrying about encountering them when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I'm off the clock and away from those buildings, I don't really dwell on it. I don't sit at home worrying that I'm going to run into one or anything like that. It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.1, F40.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because... well, the thought of seeing a rat has just gotten so bad lately. I work as a building cleaner, and I can’t even do parts of my job anymore. Even when I know it’s just an old building or a trash room, and there's probably nothing actually dangerous there, I just... I can't do it. The fear is just too much.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of the rat itself or more about the possibility of getting sick or something bad happening if you see one?\n\nPatient: I think it's really just the rat itself. I don't... I don't usually think about getting sick or catching anything from them. It's just the animal. The way they look, the way they move... just the thought of seeing one right in front of me is what terrifies me. I'm not really worried about what it's going to do to me, it's just the rat.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of other animals that are similar to rats, like mice?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I see mice sometimes down in the basements, and... well, I don't love them, but they don't bother me like that. I can keep working if I see a mouse. It's really just rats. I don't know why, but a mouse just doesn't give me that same terrifying feeling. It's strictly rats.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats started to affect other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it's just really messing up my job. I mean... I've actually had to refuse or put off cleaning assignments in certain buildings because of it. My supervisor has noticed, and he's started questioning if I can even be counted on for my regular routes anymore. I'm just really worried this is going to cost me getting better shifts, or... or a chance at a lead position. So, it's really my work life and my livelihood that are taking the biggest hit right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been afraid of rats?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two years now. It started right around the time I moved into this older apartment building. People living there just kept talking about rats being around the place, and... yeah, that's when this whole thing really began.\n\nDoctor: Can seeing pictures or videos of rats, or even just thinking about them when you're not actually around them, trigger this fear for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't like looking at them, but pictures or thinking about them doesn't give me that same panic. It's really only when I see a real one, or think there's one actually in the room with me, that I get so scared.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your social life or relationships with family and friends in any way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't really caused any problems with my friends or family. It's really just work where it's a big issue. Like I said, it's the buildings I have to clean that are the problem. My social life is pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats or worrying about encountering them when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I'm off the clock and away from those buildings, I don't really dwell on it. I don't sit at home worrying that I'm going to run into one or anything like that. It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of rats if you encountered them in a completely controlled environment, like a zoo?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I don't think that would make a difference at all. Even if it was locked up in a glass cage at a zoo, just looking at it would still make me panic. I'd still need to get away from it immediately. It doesn't really matter if the environment is controlled or not... a rat is a rat, and I just can't be anywhere near them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't... I don't usually think about getting sick or catching anything from them. It's just the animal. The way they look, the way they move... just the thought of seeing one right in front of me is what terrifies me. I'm not really worried about what it's going to do to me, it's just the rat.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of other animals that are similar to rats, like mice?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I see mice sometimes down in the basements, and... well, I don't love them, but they don't bother me like that. I can keep working if I see a mouse. It's really just rats. I don't know why, but a mouse just doesn't give me that same terrifying feeling. It's strictly rats.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats started to affect other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it's just really messing up my job. I mean... I've actually had to refuse or put off cleaning assignments in certain buildings because of it. My supervisor has noticed, and he's started questioning if I can even be counted on for my regular routes anymore. I'm just really worried this is going to cost me getting better shifts, or... or a chance at a lead position. So, it's really my work life and my livelihood that are taking the biggest hit right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been afraid of rats?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two years now. It started right around the time I moved into this older apartment building. People living there just kept talking about rats being around the place, and... yeah, that's when this whole thing really began.\n\nDoctor: Can seeing pictures or videos of rats, or even just thinking about them when you're not actually around them, trigger this fear for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't like looking at them, but pictures or thinking about them doesn't give me that same panic. It's really only when I see a real one, or think there's one actually in the room with me, that I get so scared.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your social life or relationships with family and friends in any way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't really caused any problems with my friends or family. It's really just work where it's a big issue. Like I said, it's the buildings I have to clean that are the problem. My social life is pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats or worrying about encountering them when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I'm off the clock and away from those buildings, I don't really dwell on it. I don't sit at home worrying that I'm going to run into one or anything like that. It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of rats if you encountered them in a completely controlled environment, like a zoo?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I don't think that would make a difference at all. Even if it was locked up in a glass cage at a zoo, just looking at it would still make me panic. I'd still need to get away from it immediately. It doesn't really matter if the environment is controlled or not... a rat is a rat, and I just can't be anywhere near them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats is disproportionate to the actual danger they pose?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do know that. I mean, I know most of the time they're not going to attack me or anything. But that doesn't stop the panic. It's gotten so bad that I can't even do parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like, if I have to go into a basement or a trash room in an old building, the thought of seeing one just paralyzes me. I know logically it's probably not really dangerous, but the fear is just... it's too much. That's why I'm here.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I don't... I don't usually think about getting sick or catching anything from them. It's just the animal. The way they look, the way they move... just the thought of seeing one right in front of me is what terrifies me. I'm not really worried about what it's going to do to me, it's just the rat.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of other animals that are similar to rats, like mice?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I see mice sometimes down in the basements, and... well, I don't love them, but they don't bother me like that. I can keep working if I see a mouse. It's really just rats. I don't know why, but a mouse just doesn't give me that same terrifying feeling. It's strictly rats.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats started to affect other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it's just really messing up my job. I mean... I've actually had to refuse or put off cleaning assignments in certain buildings because of it. My supervisor has noticed, and he's started questioning if I can even be counted on for my regular routes anymore. I'm just really worried this is going to cost me getting better shifts, or... or a chance at a lead position. So, it's really my work life and my livelihood that are taking the biggest hit right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been afraid of rats?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two years now. It started right around the time I moved into this older apartment building. People living there just kept talking about rats being around the place, and... yeah, that's when this whole thing really began.\n\nDoctor: Can seeing pictures or videos of rats, or even just thinking about them when you're not actually around them, trigger this fear for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't like looking at them, but pictures or thinking about them doesn't give me that same panic. It's really only when I see a real one, or think there's one actually in the room with me, that I get so scared.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your social life or relationships with family and friends in any way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't really caused any problems with my friends or family. It's really just work where it's a big issue. Like I said, it's the buildings I have to clean that are the problem. My social life is pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats or worrying about encountering them when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I'm off the clock and away from those buildings, I don't really dwell on it. I don't sit at home worrying that I'm going to run into one or anything like that. It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of rats if you encountered them in a completely controlled environment, like a zoo?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I don't think that would make a difference at all. Even if it was locked up in a glass cage at a zoo, just looking at it would still make me panic. I'd still need to get away from it immediately. It doesn't really matter if the environment is controlled or not... a rat is a rat, and I just can't be anywhere near them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats is disproportionate to the actual danger they pose?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do know that. I mean, I know most of the time they're not going to attack me or anything. But that doesn't stop the panic. It's gotten so bad that I can't even do parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like, if I have to go into a basement or a trash room in an old building, the thought of seeing one just paralyzes me. I know logically it's probably not really dangerous, but the fear is just... it's too much. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Can talking about rats with someone, when you're not actually around them, trigger your fear or make you anxious?\n\nPatient: No, just talking about them doesn't really do it. I mean, I don't love the topic, obviously, but if I'm sitting here with you and we're just talking about it, I don't feel that panic. It's more about actually being around them or thinking one is physically close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats be less intense if you knew for certain that you would never encounter one in your daily life or at work?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... if I knew for sure I'd never see one, I guess I wouldn't have to worry about it day to day. But I don't think the fear itself would go away. If one suddenly showed up, even if I was told it wouldn't happen, I'd still have the exact same reaction. So, I wouldn't be as stressed out going to work, but the actual fear of the animal wouldn't be any less intense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of rats if you encountered them in a completely controlled environment, like a zoo?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I don't think that would make a difference at all. Even if it was locked up in a glass cage at a zoo, just looking at it would still make me panic. I'd still need to get away from it immediately. It doesn't really matter if the environment is controlled or not... a rat is a rat, and I just can't be anywhere near them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats is disproportionate to the actual danger they pose?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do know that. I mean, I know most of the time they're not going to attack me or anything. But that doesn't stop the panic. It's gotten so bad that I can't even do parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like, if I have to go into a basement or a trash room in an old building, the thought of seeing one just paralyzes me. I know logically it's probably not really dangerous, but the fear is just... it's too much. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Can talking about rats with someone, when you're not actually around them, trigger your fear or make you anxious?\n\nPatient: No, just talking about them doesn't really do it. I mean, I don't love the topic, obviously, but if I'm sitting here with you and we're just talking about it, I don't feel that panic. It's more about actually being around them or thinking one is physically close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats be less intense if you knew for certain that you would never encounter one in your daily life or at work?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... if I knew for sure I'd never see one, I guess I wouldn't have to worry about it day to day. But I don't think the fear itself would go away. If one suddenly showed up, even if I was told it wouldn't happen, I'd still have the exact same reaction. So, I wouldn't be as stressed out going to work, but the actual fear of the animal wouldn't be any less intense.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you're afraid of, besides rats, that causes you a similar level of anxiety or fear?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I have normal things I don't exactly like, but nothing that makes me panic or freeze up like that. It's really just the rats. Everything else I can handle just fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's pretty much just tied to when I'm actually there, on the job, and know I have to go into a space where they might be.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats only triggered when you are in a specific situation, like at work, or can it happen at any time or place if you see a rat?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I saw a rat anywhere, I'd definitely be terrified. It's just that work is the only place I really run into them. If I saw one in my house or walking down the street, I'd still have that same panic. It's the rat itself, not just the building, you know? It's just that those old buildings at work are where they actually are.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have unwanted thoughts or images of rats pop into your mind when you're not thinking about work or cleaning?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. Like I said, when I'm not at work, I'm not really thinking about them at all. I don't get pictures of them stuck in my head or anything out of the blue. It's really just when I'm faced with them or know I have to go where they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about rats or worry that you might see one when you're doing everyday activities, like grocery shopping or walking in a park?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying, it's not something that hangs over me all the time. If I'm just at the store or taking a walk, I'm not looking over my shoulder for them. I just go about my day. It's really only an issue when I know I'm going into a situation where they usually are.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear is only triggered by actually seeing a rat, or can the fear also be triggered by other cues, such as hearing a rat or smelling something that reminds you of rats?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not just seeing them. If I hear a scratching sound near a wall, or if I just see a shadow or something moving by a pipe out of the corner of my eye, my whole body reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands get shaky, and I just feel this overwhelming urge that I have to get out of there before I can even really process what's happening. So yeah, even just hearing them sets it off instantly.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that can trigger your fear besides seeing, hearing, or thinking you might see a rat?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the things I mentioned—seeing them, hearing them scratching, or thinking one might be around. I can't really think of anything else that sets it off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of rats if you encountered them in a completely controlled environment, like a zoo?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. I don't think that would make a difference at all. Even if it was locked up in a glass cage at a zoo, just looking at it would still make me panic. I'd still need to get away from it immediately. It doesn't really matter if the environment is controlled or not... a rat is a rat, and I just can't be anywhere near them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats is disproportionate to the actual danger they pose?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do know that. I mean, I know most of the time they're not going to attack me or anything. But that doesn't stop the panic. It's gotten so bad that I can't even do parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like, if I have to go into a basement or a trash room in an old building, the thought of seeing one just paralyzes me. I know logically it's probably not really dangerous, but the fear is just... it's too much. That's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Can talking about rats with someone, when you're not actually around them, trigger your fear or make you anxious?\n\nPatient: No, just talking about them doesn't really do it. I mean, I don't love the topic, obviously, but if I'm sitting here with you and we're just talking about it, I don't feel that panic. It's more about actually being around them or thinking one is physically close to me.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats be less intense if you knew for certain that you would never encounter one in your daily life or at work?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... if I knew for sure I'd never see one, I guess I wouldn't have to worry about it day to day. But I don't think the fear itself would go away. If one suddenly showed up, even if I was told it wouldn't happen, I'd still have the exact same reaction. So, I wouldn't be as stressed out going to work, but the actual fear of the animal wouldn't be any less intense.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you're afraid of, besides rats, that causes you a similar level of anxiety or fear?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I have normal things I don't exactly like, but nothing that makes me panic or freeze up like that. It's really just the rats. Everything else I can handle just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats would be less intense if you were in a different job that didn't involve cleaning old buildings?\n\nPatient: Well, it's kind of like what I was just saying. I mean, if I worked in a nice, bright store or something instead of cleaning old buildings, I wouldn't be on edge every single shift. I wouldn't have to worry about them as much. But the actual fear? No, I don't think that would be any less intense. \n\nIf I saw a rat at a different job, I'd still completely lose it. The job just puts me in places where they hide a lot... it doesn't really change how terrified I am of the animals themselves."
